[^cnote_a]: [Transnistria](376581.md), internationally recognised as being a legal part of the [Republic of Moldova](19260.md), although *de facto* control is exercised by its internationally unrecognised government which declared independence from Moldova in 1990

[^cnote_b]: Russia is a [transcontinental country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_transcontinental_countries) spanning Eastern Europe and [North Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Asia). The vast majority of its population (80%) lives within its [European part](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Russia).

    [\[325\]](#cite_note-Vishnevsky-325)

    &#x20;However, only the population figure includes the entire state.

[^cnote_c]: [Guernsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey), the [Isle of Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Man), and [Jersey](15693.md) are [Crown Dependencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Dependencies) of the United Kingdom. Other [Channel Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_Islands) legislated by the [Bailiwick of Guernsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bailiwick_of_Guernsey) include [Alderney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alderney) and [Sark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sark).

[^cnote_d]: [Cyprus](5593.md) can be considered part of Europe or [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia); it has strong historical and sociopolitical connections with Europe. The population and area figures refer to the entire state, including the *de facto* independent part [Northern Cyprus](271409.md) which is not recognised as a sovereign nation by the vast majority of sovereign nations, nor the UN.

[^cnote_e]: Figures for [Portugal](23033.md) include the [Azores](3226.md) and [Madeira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madeira) archipelagos, both in the North Atlantic.

[^cnote_f]: Area figure for [Serbia](29265.md) includes [Kosovo](17391.md), a province that unilaterally declared its independence from Serbia on 17 February 2008, and whose sovereign status is unclear. Population and density figures are from the first results of 2011 census and are given without the disputed territory of [Kosovo](17391.md).

[^cnote_g]: Figures for [France](5843419.md) include only [metropolitan France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_France): some [politically integral parts of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_France) are geographically located outside Europe.

[^cnote_h]: [Netherlands](21148.md) population for November 2014. Population and area details include European portion only: Netherlands and three entities outside Europe ([Aruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aruba), [Curaçao](65205.md), and [Sint Maarten](9529005.md), in the [Caribbean](18956035.md)) constitute the [Kingdom of the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands). [Amsterdam](844.md) is the official capital, while [The Hague](30269.md) is the administrative seat.

[^cnote_i]: [Kazakhstan](16642.md) is physiographically considered a transcontinental country, mostly in Central Asia (UN region), partly in Eastern Europe, with European territory west of the [Ural Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ural_Mountains) and [Ural River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ural_River). However, only the population figure refers to the entire country.

[^cnote_j]: [Armenia](10918072.md) can be considered part of Eastern Europe or [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia); it has strong historical and sociopolitical connections with Europe. The population and area figures include the entire state, respectively.

[^cnote_k]: [Azerbaijan](746.md) is physiographically considered a transcontinental country, mostly in Western Asia. A small portion of its territory is located north of [Greater Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Caucasus), considered part of Eastern Europe.

    [\[326\]](#cite_note-UNstatsandCIAfacts-326)

    &#x20;However the population and area figures are for the entire state. This includes the [exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclave) of the [Nakhchivan Autonomous Republic](385358.md) and the region [Nagorno-Karabakh](65220.md).

[^cnote_l]: &#x20;[Georgia](48768.md) can be considered part of Eastern Europe or [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia);

    [\[326\]](#cite_note-UNstatsandCIAfacts-326)

    &#x20;it has strong historical and sociopolitical connections with Europe.

    [\[327\]](#cite_note-327)

    &#x20;The population and area figures include Georgian estimates for [Abkhazia](18933375.md) and [South Ossetia](318381.md), two regions that have declared and *[de facto](43594.md)* [achieved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition) independence. [International recognition](19042397.md#Positions_taken_by_states), however, is limited.

[^cnote_m]: [Turkey](11125639.md) is physiographically considered a transcontinental country, mostly in West Asia (the Middle East). Turkey has a small part of its territory (3%) in Southeast Europe called [East Thrace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Thrace).

    [\[328\]](#cite_note-328)

    &#x20;However, only the population figure includes the entire state.

[^cnote_n]: The total figures for area and population include only European portions of transcontinental countries. The precision of these figures is compromised by the ambiguous geographical extent of Europe and the lack of references for European portions of transcontinental countries.

[^cnote_o]: [Kosovo](17391.md) unilaterally declared its independence from [Serbia](29265.md) on 17 February 2008. Its sovereign status is [unclear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence). Its population is July 2009 CIA estimate.

[^cnote_p]: [Abkhazia](18933375.md) and [South Ossetia](318381.md), both of which can be considered part of Eastern Europe or [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia)

    [\[326\]](#cite_note-UNstatsandCIAfacts-326)

    &#x20;unilaterally declared their independence from [Georgia](48768.md) on 25 August 1990 and 28 November 1991, respectively. Their status as sovereign nations is [not recognised](19042397.md) by a vast majority of sovereign nations, nor the UN. Population figures stated as of 2003 census and 2000 estimates, respectively.

[^cnote_q]: [Nagorno-Karabakh](65220.md), which can be considered part of Eastern Europe or [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia), unilaterally declared its independence from [Azerbaijan](746.md) on 6 January 1992. Its status as a sovereign nation is not recognised by any sovereign nation, nor the UN. Population figures stated as of 2003 census and 2000 estimates, respectively.

[^cnote_r]: [Greenland](12118.md), an autonomous constituent country within the [Danish Realm](11746442.md), is geographically a part of the continent of North America, but has been politically and culturally associated with Europe.

[^cnote_s]: The [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md) and [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) are internationally recognised as being a legal part of [Ukraine](31750.md), although *de facto* control is exercised by governments which declared independence from Ukraine in 2014.

[^cnote_t]: Europe is normally considered its own continent in the English-speaking world, which uses the seven continent model.

    [\[329\]](#cite_note-329)

    [\[330\]](#cite_note-330)

    &#x20;Other models consider Europe as part of a Eurasian or Afro-Eurasian continent. See [Continent § Number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continent#Number) for more information.

[^cnote_u]: The map shows one of the most commonly accepted delineations of the geographical boundaries of Europe, as used by [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Society) and [Encyclopædia Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica). Whether countries are considered in Europe or Asia can vary in sources, for example in the classification of the [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook) or that of the [BBC](19344654.md). Certain countries in Europe, such as France, have [territories lying geographically outside Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_spanning_more_than_one_continent#Non-contiguous), but which are nevertheless considered integral parts of that country.

[^cnote_v]: This number includes Siberia, (about 38 million people) but excludes European Turkey (about 12 million).

[^cite_note-304]: &#x20;Go MC, Jones AR, Algee-Hewitt B, Dudzik B, Hughes C (2019). ["Classification Trends among Contemporary Filipino Crania Using Fordisc 3.1"](https://www.academia.edu/38744342). *Human Biology*. **2** (4). University of Florida Press: 293–303. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.5744/fa.2019.1005](https://doi.org/10.5744%2Ffa.2019.1005). [S2CID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S2CID_\(identifier\)) [159266278](https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID\:159266278). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20210107133718/https://www.academia.edu/38744342/Classification_Trends_Among_Modern_Filipino_Crania_Using_Fordisc_3_1) from the original on 7 January 2021. Retrieved 13 September 2020. \[Page 1] ABSTRACT: Filipinos represent a significant contemporary demographic group globally, yet they are underrepresented in the forensic anthropological literature. Given the complex population history of the Philippines, it is important to ensure that traditional methods for assessing the biological profile are appropriate when applied to these peoples. Here we analyze the classification trends of a modern Filipino sample (n = 110) when using the Fordisc 3.1 (FD3) software. We hypothesize that Filipinos represent an admixed population drawn largely from Asian and marginally from European parental gene pools, such that FD3 will classify these individuals morphometrically into reference samples that reflect a range of European admixture, in quantities from small to large. Our results show the greatest classification into Asian reference groups (72.7%), followed by Hispanic (12.7%), Indigenous American (7.3%), African (4.5%), and European (2.7%) groups included in FD3. This general pattern did not change between males and females. Moreover, replacing the raw craniometric values with their shape variables did not significantly alter the trends already observed. These classification trends for Filipino crania provide useful information for casework interpretation in forensic laboratory practice. Our findings can help biological anthropologists to better understand the evolutionary, population historical, and statistical reasons for FD3-generated classifications. The results of our studyindicate that ancestry estimation in forensic anthropology would benefit from population-focused research that gives consideration to histories of colonialism and periods of admixture.
